A day is longer\n# than any window the fleet actually uses (session, then weekly) and short\n# enough that a run stuck on a pool that never recovers fails a step rather\n# than hanging forever. ONE monotonic deadline bounds the whole wait — the\n# worst case is this budget, never a sum of per-poll timeouts.\nCAPACITY_HOLD_MAX_S = 86400.0\n\n\nclass CapacityHoldExpiredError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"The capacity a held run was waiting for never came back in budget.\n\n    Raised rather than proceeding degraded: an agent that runs on with no\n    quota emits empty turns that read as real output. Distinct from every\n    other failure in the gate so the caller can let it through the gate's\n    own best-effort ``except`` — that clause exists to keep a broken usage\n    MONITOR from failing a run, not to swallow a fleet with nothing left.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\n# Run-level quota events (status_public_*) are throttled per process:\n# parallel tasks all hit the same capacity gate at once, and one event\n# speaks for the whole run. Keyed by event kind (\"paused\" / \"resumed\")\n# so a resume isn't swallowed by the pause that preceded it.\n_QUOTA_EVENT_COOLDOWN_S = 600.0\n_last_quota_event_at: dict[str, float] = {}\n\n# Same per-process throttle idea as ``_last_quota_event_at``, for the\n# proactive credential refresh below. A dedicated global (not a shared dict\n# key) — the two cooldowns time unrelated events and must not throttle each\n# other.\n_last_proactive_refresh_at: float | None = None\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    from collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable\n    from typing import Any\n\n    from aii_lib.agent_backend._shared.models import AgentOptions\n    from aii_lib.claude_oauth.cred_manager_client import CredManagerClient\n    from aii_lib.claude_oauth.monitor import UsageMonitor\n\n\ndef _build_catch_up_prompt(\n    *,\n    cwd: Path,\n    transcript: Path | None,\n    original_prompt: str,\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Compose the prompt for a FRESH restart after a non-resumable stall.\n\n    When a turn stalls before Claude Code persisted any record,\n    ``claude --resume`` can't reload it\n    (:meth:`ReplAgent.has_resumable_transcript` is ``False``), so the\n    driver rotates to a fresh session that has ZERO memory of the task —\n    making the short :data:`_STALL_CONTINUE_NUDGE` (\"continue where you\n    left off\") useless. We instead re-issue the ORIGINAL prompt, prefixed\n    with a catch-up pointer to whatever the prior attempt left on disk: its\n    conversation transcript (when any bytes were ever written) and its\n    partial work in ``cwd``. The new session reads that to understand what\n    was underway, then continues — rather than blindly redoing it.\n\n    This is the fallback the operator asked for: \"tell it the conversation\n    so far is saved at <file>, read it to catch up, then continue\", made\n    robust to the common case where NO transcript exists (point it at the\n    on-disk partial work instead).\n    \"\"\"\n    # Opens with ``CATCH_UP_PREAMBLE_PREFIX`` so the telemetry adapters\n    # classify the whole turn as pipeline-authored. That matters most\n    # exactly here: ``original_prompt`` may BE the user's steer, and\n    # without the marker this scaffolded re-issue popped the ``\"human\"``\n    # tag and rendered the scaffold in the FE's human bubble.\n    preamble = [\n        f\"{CATCH_UP_PREAMBLE_PREFIX} before it \"\n        f\"finished, and you are a FRESH session that does not remember it.\",\n    ]\n    if transcript is not None:\n        preamble.append(\n            f\"The previous attempt's full conversation transcript is saved at \"\n            f\"{transcript} — read that file IN FULL FIRST to understand exactly \"\n            f\"what was being done and what had already been figured out, then \"\n            f\"continue from there.\"\n        )\n    preamble.append(\n        f\"Any partial work the previous attempt wrote is on disk under {cwd} — \"\n        f\"inspect that directory and REUSE whatever usable work is already there; \"\n        f\"do NOT start over from scratch if you can build on it. Then carry the \"\n        f\"task through to completion.\"\n    )\n    preamble.append(\"----- ORIGINAL TASK BELOW -----\")\n    return \"\\n\\n\".join(preamble) + \"\\n\\n\" + original_prompt\n\n\n# Opening turn when a task continues on a new worker pod in the lost pod's\n# session (``AgentOptions.resume_session_id``). Opens with\n# ``STALL_CONTINUE_NUDGE_PREFIX`` so the telemetry adapters classify it as\n# pipeline-authored, like the stall nudge above.\n_POD_RESUME_NUDGE = (\n    f\"{STALL_CONTINUE_NUDGE_PREFIX} — the machine this task was running on was \"\n    f\"replaced, and this is the same conversation, reloaded. Reuse the work \"\n    f\"already written to disk. Do NOT start over.\"\n)\n\n\n#: Joins a revised task onto :data:`_POD_RESUME_NUDGE`.\n_POD_RESUME_REVISED_TASK = (\n    \"The task's instructions were revised after this conversation received \"\n    \"them. Where the version below differs from the one above, follow the \"\n    \"version below; the work already done stands.\\n\\n\"\n    \"----- REVISED TASK BELOW -----\"\n)\n\n\ndef _pod_resume_nudge(revised: list[str]) -> str:\n    \"\"\":data:`_POD_RESUME_NUDGE`, carrying the current wording of ``revised``.\"\"\"\n    if not revised:\n        return _POD_RESUME_NUDGE\n    return \"\\n\\n\".join([_POD_RESUME_NUDGE, _POD_RESUME_REVISED_TASK, *revised])\n\n\ndef _resume_lost_session(repl: ReplAgent, prompts: list[str]) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"The prompts that continue a lost pod's session instead of restarting it.\n\n    ``repl`` was built with ``--resume <the lost session>``. That id is the\n    task's identity: the orchestrator read it from THIS task's workspace\n    (``aii_lib.run.worker_continuity``) and the transcript sits in this cwd's\n    project bucket, so whether it is this task's session is never asked of\n    the prompt text. A redeploy that rewords the prompt templates (on\n    2026-09-23 one paragraph of every gen_art prompt changed) must not turn a\n    two-hour session into a fresh one. Three cases:\n\n    * No transcript on disk: nothing to continue. The prompts are unchanged,\n      and :meth:`ReplAgent.start` rotates to a fresh session on its own.\n    * A transcript without a single typed prompt (a startup dialog ate the\n      paste): the task never reached it. A fresh session, told to read the\n      old transcript first.\n    * Otherwise resume it with a short nudge. The prompts up to the last one\n      the transcript holds verbatim were delivered, and only the ones after\n      it are still sent; with none held verbatim the first was delivered in\n      an earlier wording. Every delivered prompt whose wording changed rides\n      the nudge, so the revised instructions still arrive.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not prompts or not repl.has_resumable_transcript():\n        return prompts\n    if not repl.transcript_holds_typed_prompt():\n        transcript = repl.restart_fresh()\n        catch_up = _build_catch_up_prompt(\n            cwd=repl.cwd, transcript=transcript, original_prompt=prompts[0]\n        )\n        return [catch_up, *prompts[1:]]\n    verbatim = [repl.transcript_carries_prompt(p) for p in prompts]\n    last = max((i for i, held in enumerate(verbatim) if held), default=0)\n    revised = [prompts[i] for i in range(last + 1) if not verbatim[i]]\n    return [_pod_resume_nudge(revised), *prompts[last + 1 :]]\n\n\ndef _stall_retry_prompt(\n    *,\n    prompt: str,\n    is_human_turn: bool,\n    transcript_has_prompt: bool,\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"What to re-drive after a no-progress stall + ``claude --resume``.\n\n    The nudge is only correct when the reloaded conversation ALREADY holds\n    the prompt that stalled — then re-pasting it would duplicate a possibly\n    huge message for nothing. Both other cases must re-send it verbatim:\n\n    * ``is_human_turn`` — a fork steer or hot-resume inject. It stalled\n      before Claude persisted it, so the reload does not contain it and the\n      nudge would silently drop the user's instruction.\n    * ``not transcript_has_prompt`` — the session persisted SOMETHING, so\n      ``--resume`` works, but not our prompt. This is run_u8XZzUX3iMpN\n      (2026-09-06): a startup dialog ate the paste, the CLI wrote its own\n      records anyway, and the file-exists test read that as \"the task is in\n      there\". The resumed agent got \"continue where you left off\" as the\n      first user message in its transcript and spent Opus turns\n      reverse-engineering its task from the pipeline's prompt templates.\n      \"Is there a file\" and \"did my prompt reach the model\" are different\n      questions; only the second one licenses the nudge.\n    \"\"\"\n    if is_human_turn or not transcript_has_prompt:\n        return prompt\n    return _STALL_CONTINUE_NUDGE\n\n\ndef _repl_stall_restart_prompt(\n    *,\n    stalled_for_s: int,\n    resumed: bool,\n    original_prompt: str,\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"The opening turn of the ONE whole-run retry after a REPL stall.\n\n    :func:`_stall_retry_prompt` above covers a stall inside one drive, under\n    the ``seq_prompt_retries`` budget. This is the layer after that budget is\n    spent: the ``TimeoutError`` reached ``run()``'s outer handler, which used\n    to fail the task outright — so a stalled gen_art step never reached the\n    struct-out, expected-files or ``post_validate`` retries sitting right\n    below it (one of the three terminal gen_art failures on prod run B).\n\n    The turn SAYS WHAT HAPPENED, because the agent cannot infer it: from its\n    side a restarted session simply picks up mid-thought, and an agent that\n    does not know it was interrupted re-plans from the top and redoes work\n    already on disk.\n\n    ``resumed`` distinguishes the two restarts the caller can have performed:\n\n    * ``True``  — :meth:`ReplAgent.restart_resumed`; the conversation above\n      the turn is the agent's own, so pointing at it is enough.\n    * ``False`` — :meth:`ReplAgent.restart_fresh`; the session remembers\n      NOTHING, so the original task has to travel with the turn, the same way\n      :func:`_build_catch_up_prompt` carries it on the in-drive fresh path.\n\n    Opens with :data:`REPL_STALL_RESTART_PREFIX` so the telemetry adapters\n    classify it as pipeline-authored and it cannot pop the ``\"human\"`` tag\n    queued for the user's real steer.\n    \"\"\"\n    opening = (\n        f\"{REPL_STALL_RESTART_PREFIX} for {stalled_for_s}s and was restarted; \"\n        f\"continue from your last step.\"\n    )\n    if resumed:\n        return (\n            f\"{opening} The conversation above is your own — reuse whatever \"\n            f\"partial work is already written to disk and do NOT start over.\"\n        )\n    return \"\\n\\n\".join(\n        [\n            f\"{opening} You are a FRESH session that does not remember the \"\n            f\"conversation, but any partial work the stalled attempt wrote is \"\n            f\"still on disk in your workspace — inspect it and REUSE it rather \"\n            f\"than redoing it.\",\n            \"----- ORIGINAL TASK BELOW -----\",\n            original_prompt,\n        ]\n    )\n\n\ndef _corrective_feedback(options: AgentOptions, error: str, attempt: int) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Corrective-turn prompt for a missing, invalid or uncheckable submission.\n\n    Carries the caller's ``force_output_prompt`` after the validation error:\n    it is the caller's own wording for \"submit now\" (gen_report_text names\n    its draft file there), and this backend used to drop it.\n    \"\"\"\n    feedback = build_validation_feedback(\n        Path(_STRUCT_OUT_REF), error, attempt, output_file=_STRUCT_OUT_REF\n    )\n    force = options.force_output_prompt\n    return f\"{feedback}\\n\\n{force}\" if force else feedback\n\n\nasync def validate_expected_files_on_repl(\n    *,\n    options: AgentOptions,\n    structured_output: dict,\n    struct_out_path: Path | None,\n    struct_schema: dict | None,\n    drive_turn: Callable[[str], Awaitable[Any]],\n) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Expected-files existence check + corrective turns, on a REPL session.\n\n    Wires this backend into the SHARED helper\n    :func:`aii_lib.agent_backend._shared.core.expected_files.validate_and_retry_expected_files`\n    — the same one ``sdk_openhands_agent/_agent/_struct_out.py`` calls — so the\n    budget (``max_expected_files_retries``), the feedback text and the two\n    ``emit.status_public_warning`` lines are literally one implementation\n    rather than two that drift.\n\n    Until this existed, ``terminal_claude_agent`` never read\n    ``expected_files_struct_out_field`` at all, although ``experiment.py``\n    passes ``expected_files_field=\"out_expected_files\"`` through\n    ``build_options``. A step could therefore end schema-valid having written\n    no files: ``gen_art_experiment_2`` on prod run B did exactly that and was\n    caught only post hoc, by ``_find_experiment_files`` reporting \"No\n    experiment output files found in workspace\" with no turn left to fix it.\n\n    ``drive_turn`` runs one corrective turn on the SAME session; the struct-out\n    file is re-read after it, since the agent's fix normally rewrites it.\n    \"\"\"\n    prompt_results = [PromptResult(\"\", \"\", 0, structured_output)]\n\n    async def execute_prompt_fn(\n        prompt: str,\n        *,\n        # Part of the shared helper's call contract; this backend's turn\n        # driver always re-reads the struct-out file, so there is nothing\n        # to switch on.\n        with_output_format: bool = True,\n    ) -> tuple[PromptResult, None]:\n        await drive_turn(prompt)\n        data, _ = (\n            validate_struct_out(struct_out_path, struct_schema)\n            if struct_out_path is not None\n            else (None, None)\n        )\n        return PromptResult(\"\", \"\", 0, data), None\n\n    return await validate_and_retry_expected_files(\n        options, prompt_results, execute_prompt_fn=execute_prompt_fn\n    )\n\n\ndef _hold_budget_s(resets_at: str | None) -> float:\n    \"\"\"One monotonic budget for the whole capacity wait, in seconds.\n\n    A known reset plus :data:`_CAPACITY_HOLD_SLACK_S`, so the wait outlives\n    the moment the window is said to clear by more than one monitor poll;\n    :data:`CAPACITY_HOLD_MAX_S` when nothing says, or when the stamp is in\n    the past or unparsable (both mean \"we do not actually know\").\n    \"\"\"\n    if resets_at:\n        try:\n            when = datetime.fromisoformat(resets_at)\n        except ValueError:\n            # WHY: an unreadable stamp means the reset is unknown, which the\n            # fallback below already handles — the full-day budget. Failing\n            # here would give the wait no budget at all.\n            when = None\n        if when is not None:\n            if when.tzinfo is None:\n                when = when.replace(tzinfo=UTC)\n            budget = when - datetime.now(UTC) + timedelta(seconds=_CAPACITY_HOLD_SLACK_S)\n            if budget.total_seconds() > 0:\n                return min(budget.total_seconds(), CAPACITY_HOLD_MAX_S)\n    return CAPACITY_HOLD_MAX_S\n\n\ndef request_claude_capacity_hold(*, detail: str, resets_at: str | None) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Ask for the current run to be paused until Claude capacity returns.\n\n    Returns the run id the hold was recorded against, or ``None`` when there\n    is no run to hold (no workflow in context) or the write failed. Never\n    raises: a hold is how the run gets rescued, not a precondition for it —\n    a database blip must not turn a quota pause into a crash.\n\n    The run id is the ROOT pipeline run, not this agent's task: the dashboard\n    stops and resumes RUNS, and every parallel task under one run shares the\n    single hold (:func:`aii_lib.run.capacity_holds.request_hold` is\n    idempotent, which is what makes the shared row safe).\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        from dbos import DBOS\n\n        from aii_lib.run.capacity_holds import CLAUDE_SUBJECT, request_hold\n        from aii_lib.run.node_id import root_run_id\n\n        run_id = root_run_id(DBOS.workflow_id or \"\")\n        if not run_id:\n            return None\n        request_hold(\n            run_id=run_id,\n            backend=\"terminal_claude_agent\",\n            subject=CLAUDE_SUBJECT,\n            detail=detail,\n            resets_at=_parse_reset(resets_at),\n        )\n    except Exception:\n        logger.opt(exception=True).warning(\n            \"terminal_claude_agent: could not record a capacity hold — the run \"\n            \"will not be paused automatically\"\n        )\n        return None\n    logger.warning(f\"terminal_claude_agent: capacity hold requested for {run_id} — {detail}\")\n    return run_id\n\n\ndef clear_claude_capacity_hold(run_id: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Drop the hold ``run_id`` placed — capacity came back before the server acted.\n\n    A RELEASE, not a clear: if the server already claimed the run for a stop,\n    the row stays, because it is what the supervisor resumes the cancelled\n    run from (run_pv3kwdDbI8Lf).\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        from aii_lib.run.capacity_holds import release_hold\n\n        if not release_hold(run_id):\n            logger.info(\n                f\"terminal_claude_agent: the server is already stopping {run_id} — \"\n                \"its hold stays for the resume\"\n            )\n    except Exception:\n        logger.opt(exception=True).warning(\n            f\"terminal_claude_agent: could not clear the capacity hold for {run_id}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _parse_reset(resets_at: str | None) -> datetime | None:\n    \"\"\"An ISO reset stamp as an aware UTC datetime; ``None`` when unknown.\n\n    An unparsable stamp is the same answer as an absent one — we do not know\n    when capacity returns — and ``None`` is exactly how the hold row spells\n    that. Raising instead would turn a cosmetic reading problem into a failed\n    hold, which is the one write that rescues the run.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not resets_at:\n        return None\n    try:\n        when = datetime.fromisoformat(resets_at)\n    except ValueError:\n        # WHY: a stamp we cannot read is a reset we do not know — the same\n        # state as no stamp at all. Logged at the source that produced it.\n        return None\n    return when.replace(tzinfo=UTC) if when.tzinfo is None else when.astimezone(UTC)\n\n\nasync def await_claude_capacity(monitor: UsageMonitor) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Wait for Claude-Max quota capacity; hold the run when waiting is not enough.\n\n    Returns how long the wait took. Waits :data:`_CAPACITY_GRACE_S` first —\n    the monitor rotates accounts on its own and most waits end inside that.\n    Past the grace the fleet is genuinely empty, so this records a capacity\n    hold and keeps waiting: the dashboard's supervisor stops the run and\n    resumes it when the window clears, and if capacity returns here first the\n    hold is dropped and the turn simply continues.\n\n    Raises :class:`CapacityHoldExpiredError` when the whole budget elapses without\n    capacity — deliberately, in place of the \"proceed best-effort\" branch this\n    replaced, which let a run grind on against a fleet with nothing left.\n    \"\"\"\n    started = time.monotonic()\n    if await monitor.async_wait_for_capacity(timeout=_CAPACITY_GRACE_S):\n        return time.monotonic() - started\n\n    resets_at = _monitor_weekly_reset(monitor)\n    detail = (\n        \"Every Claude account is over its usage threshold — the run has no \"\n        \"quota left to make progress on.\"\n    )\n    run_id = request_claude_capacity_hold(detail=detail, resets_at=resets_at)\n\n    # ONE monotonic deadline for the whole wait, so its worst case is the\n    # budget rather than the sum of however many polls it takes.\n    deadline = time.monotonic() + _hold_budget_s(resets_at)\n    while (remaining := deadline - time.monotonic()) > 0:\n        if await monitor.async_wait_for_capacity(timeout=remaining):\n            if run_id:\n                clear_claude_capacity_hold(run_id)\n            return time.monotonic() - started\n    raise CapacityHoldExpiredError(\n        f\"Claude quota capacity did not return within {_hold_budget_s(resets_at) / 3600:.1f}h \"\n        f\"of the hold — refusing to run the turn without quota.\"\n    )\n\n\n# A ``403`` on the Claude-Max OAuth path is quota exhaustion, not a wrong\n# credential — the CLI reports \"usage limit reached\" / \"quota\" / \"exceeded\"\n# rather than an auth problem, and the fleet-wide rotation has nowhere left to\n# go. It is deliberately absent from ``_RETRIABLE_API_ERROR_STATUSES``: no\n# number of ``\"continue\"`` retries buys quota. Classified by MESSAGE as well as\n# status so an unrelated 403 (a permission problem, say) does not pause a run\n# that a retry could have saved.\n_QUOTA_403_MARKERS = (\"quota\", \"usage limit\", \"limit reached\", \"exceeded\", \"out of\")\n\n\ndef _is_quota_exhausted_403(status: int | None, message: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True iff ``status``/``message`` are Claude reporting exhausted quota.\"\"\"\n    if status != 403:\n        return False\n    m = (message or \"\").lower()\n    return any(marker in m for marker in _QUOTA_403_MARKERS)\n\n\ndef _monitor_weekly_reset(monitor: UsageMonitor | None = None) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"The usage monitor's weekly-reset reading, or ``None`` when it has none.\n\n    ``monitor`` is the one the caller already holds; omitting it falls back to\n    the process singleton, which is what the 403 path needs — that runs on the\n    REPL-drive executor thread, where nothing else in scope knows the answer.\n    Asking the passed monitor when there is one matters: a caller that waited\n    on THAT monitor must not stamp a hold with a different one's reading.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        if monitor is None:\n            from aii_lib.claude_oauth import get_monitor\n\n            monitor = get_monitor()\n        return monitor.weekly_resets_at()\n    except Exception:\n        logger.opt(exception=True).debug(\"terminal_claude_agent: no weekly reset reading\")\n        return None\n\n\ndef _quota_event_ok(kind: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"At most one ``kind`` quota event per cooldown window.\"\"\"\n    now = time.monotonic()\n    last = _last_quota_event_at.get(kind)\n    # ``None`` (never seen) must always pass: ``time.monotonic()``'s zero\n    # point is arbitrary (system boot), so a numeric sentinel like ``0.0``\n    # wrongly reads as \"an event at t=0\" and throttles the first event on a\n    # freshly-booted host where ``now < cooldown``.\n    if last is not None and now - last < _QUOTA_EVENT_COOLDOWN_S:\n        return False\n    _last_quota_event_at[kind] = now\n    return True\n\n\ndef _proactive_refresh_ok() -> bool:\n    \"\"\"At most one proactive near-expiry refresh per cooldown window.\n\n    Same ``None``-sentinel reasoning as :func:`_quota_event_ok` — a first\n    call must always pass regardless of ``time.monotonic()``'s arbitrary\n    zero point.\n    \"\"\"\n    global _last_proactive_refresh_at\n    now = time.monotonic()\n    if (\n        _last_proactive_refresh_at is not None\n        and now - _last_proactive_refresh_at < _PROACTIVE_REFRESH_COOLDOWN_S\n    ):\n        return False\n    _last_proactive_refresh_at = now\n    return True\n\n\ndef maybe_refresh_near_expiry_credentials(\n    *,\n    client: CredManagerClient | None = None,\n    refresh: Callable[[int], bool] | None = None,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Refresh credentials BEFORE a session launches if the active slot is ``near_expiry``.\n\n    Evidence (2026-09-20, two prod orchestrator-pod runs): the cred manager\n    reported the active slot ``near_expiry`` from 12:46Z; nothing refreshed\n    proactively, so the token was still served until it was actually\n    rejected — at ~13:10Z both runs hit an auth 401 mid-step, killing 3\n    running subagents whose work then had to be redone before the existing\n    reactive repair (:data:`_FORCE_REFRESH_MIN_VALIDITY_S`, this module's\n    401 handler) refreshed the token and the run recovered. Calling the\n    SAME refresh here, while the slot still reads ``near_expiry`` instead of\n    after a request has already failed on the dead token, avoids that dead\n    window entirely.\n\n    No-op when the central cred manager is disabled (``cred_manager.enabled``\n    off — the pre-existing ability-server-only path has no ``near_expiry``\n    concept to poll) or when the slot isn't ``near_expiry``. The status\n    check itself (``GET /v1/accounts``) is cheap and side-effect free, so it\n    runs on every call; only the refresh call is cooldown-gated\n    (:func:`_proactive_refresh_ok`) so several steps racing to launch at\n    once don't each pay for a refresh round trip.\n\n    Best-effort like the reactive path: ANY failure here (manager\n    unreachable, refresh call raising) is logged as a WARNING and swallowed\n    — this must never block a session from launching, and the reactive\n    auth-401 repair remains the backstop if a stale token slips through.\n\n    ``client``/``refresh`` are injected by tests only; real callers omit\n    both and get the live manager client + :func:`ensure_oauth_token_fresh`\n    (mirrors the ``monitor`` param on :func:`_monitor_weekly_reset` above).\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        from aii_lib.claude_oauth import cred_manager_client\n\n        settings = cred_manager_client.load_cred_manager_settings()\n        if not settings.enabled:\n            return\n        active_client = (\n            client if client is not None else cred_manager_client.CredManagerClient(settings)\n        )\n        status = active_client.active_slot_status()\n        if status != \"near_expiry\":\n            return\n        if not _proactive_refresh_ok():\n            return\n\n        refresh_fn = refresh\n        if refresh_fn is None:\n            from aii_lib.claude_oauth.autologin import ensure_oauth_token_fresh\n\n            refresh_fn = ensure_oauth_token_fresh\n\n        if refresh_fn(_FORCE_REFRESH_MIN_VALIDITY_S):\n            logger.info(\"terminal_claude_agent: proactive credential refresh (slot near_expiry)\")\n        else:\n            logger.warning(\n                \"terminal_claude_agent: proactive credential refresh failed \"\n                \"(slot near_expiry) — falling back to the reactive 401 path\"\n            )\n    except Exception as e:\n        logger.warning(\n            f\"terminal_claude_agent: proactive near_expiry check failed \"\n            f\"({type(e).__name__}: {e}) — falling back to the reactive 401 path\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _coerce_prompts(prompts: str | list[str]) -> list[str]:\n    if isinstance(prompts, str):\n        return [prompts]\n    return list(prompts)\n\n\ndef _maybe_append_struct_out_instruction(\n    prompt_list: list[str],\n    prompt_sources: list[str],\n    schema: dict[str, Any],\n    *,\n    struct_out_filename: str = _STRUCT_OUT_FILENAME,\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Append the JSON output-format instruction to the last prompt unless it is human-sourced.\n\n    The instruction tells the model where to write its result. It must NOT be\n    glued onto a ``/fork`` or hot-resume message (a ``\"human\"`` entry in\n    ``prompt_sources``), which must render verbatim in the FE human bubble. A resumed session already\n    carries the original instruction in its history; a fresh fork with no history\n    is re-taught the file requirement by the caller's post-validation retry loop.\n    Mid-flight injects already bypass this append, so gating here makes BOTH human\n    paths consistent — the user always sees exactly what they typed.\n\n    Pure: returns a (possibly new) list; the input is never mutated.\n    \"\"\"\n    if \"human\" in prompt_sources:\n        return prompt_list\n    out = list(prompt_list)\n    out[-1] = out[-1] + build_struct_out_instruction(schema, f\"./{struct_out_filename}\")\n    return out\n\n\ndef _options_to_repl(options: AgentOptions, *, resume_session_id: str = \"\") -> ReplAgent:\n    \"\"\"Map :class:`AgentOptions` → :class:`ReplAgent` constructor args.\n\n    ``resume_session_id`` is non-empty only on the fork-replay path\n    (the caller looked up the parent task's captured session id in\n    :attr:`ResumeContext.fork_session_ids`). When set, the REPL is\n    spawned via ``claude --resume <id>`` so the model rehydrates the\n    parent's full conversation history before the first new turn.\n    \"\"\"\n    cwd = Path(options.cwd or \".\").resolve()\n\n    mcp_config: dict | None = None\n    if isinstance(options.mcp_servers, dict) and options.mcp_servers:\n        if \"mcpServers\" in options.mcp_servers:\n            mcp_config = options.mcp_servers\n        else:\n            mcp_config = {\"mcpServers\": options.mcp_servers}\n\n    system_prompt = options.system_prompt if isinstance(options.system_prompt, str) else None\n    # Fold the write-containment rail in behind the step's persona. The driver\n    # emits ``system_prompt`` as the ``<system-prompt>`` block on turn 0, ahead\n    # of the scaffold, so this is where the rail lands on this backend — one\n    # place, from ``AgentOptions.workspace_rail`` (stamped at ``build_options``\n    # from ``cwd``), never from a prompt module. A preset-dict system prompt\n    # leaves ``system_prompt`` None above, so the rail then stands alone rather\n    # than being dropped.\n    rail = (getattr(options, \"workspace_rail\", \"\") or \"\").strip()\n    if rail:\n        system_prompt = f\"{system_prompt.strip()}\\n\\n{rail}\" if system_prompt else rail\n    # A model with no effort dial (Haiku) takes NO ``--effort`` at all: the\n    # CLI errors when handed one, and every config layer above writes a plain\n    # string because ``AgentConfig.effort`` cannot hold a null. So the dial is\n    # dropped here, at the one place an id and an effort meet the process,\n    # rather than trusted to whichever preset, overlay or test wrote the pair.\n    effort = options.effort if options.effort in {\"low\", \"medium\", \"high\", \"xhigh\", \"max\"} else None\n    if NO_EFFORT_MODEL_MARKER in (options.model or \"\").lower():\n        effort = None\n    allowed = list(options.allowed_tools) if options.allowed_tools else None\n    disallowed = list(options.disallowed_tools) if options.disallowed_tools else None\n    setting_sources = list(options.setting_sources) if options.setting_sources else [\"project\"]\n\n    # ``max_turn_wait_secs`` is the REPL driver's NO-PROGRESS (stall)\n    # window: as long as new JSONL records keep arriving, the deadline\n    # rolls forward, so legitimate long-running research turns never\n    # false-positive. We trip only when the model emits NOTHING for\n    # this long — true stuck / hung / crashed-REPL signal.\n    #\n    # ``seq_prompt_timeout`` is the caller's per-turn knob. We honor it\n    # when set; ``None`` (no caller value) falls through to 600s — long\n    # enough to ride out a slow web tool, short enough to surface a\n    # genuinely wedged process. NOT confused with ``agent_timeout``,\n    # which is the absolute wall-clock cap on the WHOLE agent run\n    # (orthogonal concept — enforced at the orchestrator layer above).\n    no_progress_secs = (\n        options.seq_prompt_timeout\n        if options.seq_prompt_timeout is not None\n        else _DEFAULT_NO_PROGRESS_S\n    )\n\n    # Overall agent time budget → a single deadline the per-tool PostToolUse\n    # time-remaining hook counts down from. Mirror the OpenHands injector's\n    # source (agent / container timeout); take the TIGHTER of the two that are\n    # set. ``None`` when neither is set ⇒ no hook installed (no budget).\n    budgets = [\n        b for b in (options.agent_timeout, options.container_timeout) if b is not None and b > 0\n    ]\n    deadline_epoch = (time.time() + min(budgets)) if budgets else None\n\n    return ReplAgent(\n        cwd=cwd,\n        model=options.model,\n        effort=effort,\n        system_prompt=system_prompt,\n        allowed_tools=allowed,\n        disallowed_tools=disallowed,\n        setting_sources=setting_sources,\n        mcp_config=mcp_config,\n        permission_mode=options.permission_mode or \"bypassPermissions\",\n        max_turn_wait_secs=float(no_progress_secs),\n        resume_session_id=resume_session_id,\n        time_remaining_deadline_epoch=deadline_epoch,\n        # Per-tier subagent definitions. Only written when efforts were\n        # resolved for this step (``build_options`` stamps both together, or\n        # neither); without them delegation keeps naming ``model=<id>`` in the\n        # priming text and needs no definitions at all.\n        subagent_tier_models=dict(options.subagent_tier_models or {}),\n        subagent_tier_efforts=dict(options.subagent_tier_efforts or {}),\n    )\n", "numLines": 813, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 813}}

The research questions are RQ1 (which temporal network indicators anticipate emergence across domains) and RQ2 (how concepts diffuse across disciplines). The target venue is *Applied Network Science*, collection \"Networks for everyday life\".\n\n**Revised main hypothesis.** A new concept becomes broadly and durably integrated when the fields that adopt it start citing its literature the way they cite their own. We measure this as the **naturalisation gap A\\*_h**: the concept's lineage log-odds ratio across discipline layers (citing layer × cited layer, off-home vs home) minus the same citing papers' log-odds ratio on their *other* references.\n- In the concept term, availability and preferential attachment cancel, because home and off-home adopters face the same concept stock.\n- Subtracting the background term removes general disciplinary citation homophily. The background term acts as a negative-control exposure.\n- Links between papers that share an author are removed.\n\n## What changed after the review\n\n| Critique | Change |\n|---|---|\n| Uniform availability null ignores homophily, preferential attachment and self-citation | The headline estimator is now A\\*_h (above), with self-citations separated. The old A\\* and an impact-aware A\\* are kept as foils, and the M1 decomposition is pre-registered. |\n| Outcome-conditioned sampling frame | Frame N adds outcome-blind title n-grams. Size filters use years ≤ t0 only. Wikipedia enters O5 by creation date only. Frame W is reweighted by inverse inclusion probability if needed. |\n| Volume-dependent O2 | Primary breadth is O2r (rarefied field richness, m = 50). Off-home volume and field composition are added to the baseline. |\n| Author-profile leakage | Features use venue labels (with a drift audit). Outcomes use author labels. A leakage-free team profile is the sensitivity check. |\n| P4 detection-power asymmetry | One change-point detector is calibrated to a 5% false-alarm rate for all series. Panel lead-lag tests and a permutation placebo are added, plus sensitivity at 10, 15 and 25 minimum links. |\n| Wrong OpenAlex prices | Costs were measured from response headers (below), and the budget was recomputed (about 45k credits in total). |\n| Weak probe evidence | A new probe runs the real estimator on 8 phrase-grounded concepts. |\n| Concept centrality not concept-level; O3 window | A concept-level backbone is built. O3 now uses t0+3..t0+8. C1 and C2 are primary; C3–C6 are secondary with Holm correction. |\n| Novelty framing | A\\*_h is described as a concept-conditional, background-adjusted self-citation (layer-assortativity) index. Rinia 2002 and Yan et al. 2013 are cited. |\n\n## Probe findings (`probes/`, about $0.10 of OpenAlex quota)\n\n- **Homophily.** Background homophily log-odds ratio is 0.55–3.26 (median about 1.1). It equals or exceeds the raw concept-lineage log-odds ratio (0.16–3.65) in 6 of 8 concepts.\n- **Early adoption is borrowed.** A\\*_h in the first 5 years is negative in 6 of 8 concepts: iPS cells −0.63 [−1.05, −0.17], extreme learning machine −1.06, crowdsourcing +0.38, compressed sensing +0.24.\n- **Impact null and self-citation.** The impact-aware null shifts the uniform A\\* by −0.29 to +0.85. Author self-citations are 9–21% of lineage links.\n- **Grounding.** The exact-string share of stemmed phrase matches is 0.35–0.97. \"altmetrics\" returns about 3,700 works a year in 2000. Venue labels cover 26–80% of concept-papers. The strict newborn rule rejects 6 of 8 known new concepts.\n- **Measured OpenAlex costs.** A `group_by` call costs 1 credit, even with a `title_and_abstract.search` filter. Paged search costs 10 credits per 200 works. An ID batch costs 1 credit per 50 works. Singletons are free. The free allowance is 10k credits a day.\n\n## Layout\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|---|---|\n| `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json` | The deliverable: hypothesis, motivation, assumptions, plan, success criteria, related work, terms, 4 alternates |\n| `build_output.py` | Writes the deliverable JSON (all text lives here) |\n| `probes/probe_null_decomposition.py` | OpenAlex probe: phrase grounding, venue labels, lineage links, A\\*_unif, A\\*_imp, self-citation share, concept and background log-odds ratios, A\\*_h with bootstrap CI |\n| `probes/probe_null_rows.json` | Per-concept results (7 concepts; the topological-insulator run failed on an over-long ID batch, since fixed) |\n| `probes/probe_topological_insulator_first_run.json` | Topological-insulator row from the first launch (same estimator) |\n| `probes/probe_null_out.txt` | Raw console log. Partly interleaved with an aborted first launch; `probe_null_rows.json` is authoritative |\n| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage manifest (empty: no heavy files) |\n\n## How to run\n\n```bash\nexport OPENALEX_API_KEY=...                      # the run's OpenAlex key (not stored here)\npython3 probes/probe_null_decomposition.py        # needs requests; about 1k credits\npython3 build_output.py                           # regenerates the deliverable JSON\n```\n\n## Restoring removed files\n\nNothing is marked `delete`. This step created no caches, downloads or binaries.\n", "numLines": 55, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 55}}

  "domain_practice": "How co-authorship-based predictors of idea/concept diffusion are studied in scientometrics and science-of-science. (1) Reference studies. Cheng, Smith, Ren, Cao, Smith & McFarland (2023, American Sociological Review 88(3), 'How New Ideas Diffuse in Science'; DOI 10.1177/00031224231166955): about 60,000 new ideas (phrases) introduced in Web of Science from 1993 to 2016, followed across 38M later papers. Ideas become 'core concepts' when they reach 'expansive networks of unrelated authors', achieve consistent usage, co-occur with prominent ideas and fit existing traditions. I read the abstract and the publisher summary only; the full text was paywalled (HTTP 403), so the exact operationalisation of 'unrelated authors' is NOT verified here and the executor must not claim an exact replication. Bettencourt, Kaiser & Kaur (2009, Journal of Informetrics 3(3):210-221, 'Scientific discovery and topological transitions in collaboration networks') is the opposing structural expectation: across 8 fields, a field's co-authorship graph goes from many small disconnected groups to a giant connected component as the field matures ('shared concepts and tools knit practitioners together'). Cheng et al. therefore predict that MORE independent groups early on means wider later spread, while Bettencourt et al. predict that consolidation (fewer groups, a giant component) accompanies successful field formation. The screen must let the data choose the sign and report it per fold. Other standard background: Newman (2001, PNAS) on co-authorship network structure; Rotolo, Hicks & Martin (2015, Research Policy) on the five attributes of emergence and the lack of a ground truth; Small, Boyack & Klavans (2014, Research Policy) on emerging-topic detection with citation/co-occurrence structure. (2) Baselines every comparable paper reports: raw volume and growth of the concept, and the number of distinct adopting authors. Any component-count measure scales almost mechanically with adopter count and team size, so readers expect at minimum a size control (adopter count, papers) and preferably a null that preserves team sizes and author productivity. Mean team size is a known confounder: large-team fields (genomics consortia, clinical trials) have few, very large components. (3) Data conventions: author disambiguation is the main measurement risk. OpenAlex author IDs are algorithmic, and ID splitting inflates component counts (makes 'unrelated' groups out of one person) while merging deflates them, so papers report a sensitivity check with a coarser name-based identity (surname + first initial). Hyperauthorship (Cronin 2001): papers with more than about 25-50 authors are either excluded or down-weighted in co-authorship analyses, and both versions are reported. OpenAlex list responses truncate authorships at 100 authors (flag is_authors_truncated); full lists are available by singleton lookup. (4) Sample sizes and statistics: idea-level diffusion studies with tens of thousands of ideas use regression and survival models; small curated panels (tens of concepts, as here) are accepted only for comparing candidates against each other with resampling CIs and effect-size margins, never for absolute claims. Concept-level bootstrap and grouped (leave-one-field-out) cross-validation are the norm for transfer claims; Spearman correlations and AUCs are the usual reporting units. Sources read: the Cheng et al. abstract (Stanford GSB and SAGE listing), the Bettencourt et al. abstract (ScienceDirect/ResearchGate listing), the OpenAlex Help Center example-costs page, and the run's iter_3 gen_hypo README with its probe cost measurements.",

Base https://api.openalex.org, api_key param = the key from the user's original request (read it from env OPENALEX_API_KEY, falling back to the constant in the prompt; never write it into outputs or logs). get(url_params) -> check disk cache first (NEVER re-query a cached request). Otherwise send the request, parse the x-ratelimit-* headers (credits used/remaining and credit_costs if present), and add the measured cost to a persistent ledger (cache/credit_ledger.jsonl: time, endpoint, kind, cost, remaining). Guards: HARD_CAP = 1200 credits for this artifact (sum of measured costs); STOP_REMAINING = 1000 (if the header shows remaining < 1000, stop all new paid calls); 429/5xx -> exponential backoff (1,2,4,...,60 s, max 6 tries). Concurrency: at most 4 paid requests in flight and at most 8 free singletons. Cost model used for projection BEFORE each call: group_by = 1; list+filter page = 1; list page with title_and_abstract.search = 10; singleton = 0. After the first 5 calls of each kind, replace the model with the measured header costs, and abort with a clear message if a kind costs more than 2x the model.\n\n2. GLOBAL DENOMINATOR (1 credit): /works?filter=type:article|review,is_paratext:false&group_by=publication_year -> total_works[y].\n\n3. GROUNDING for each concept c in order (78 x 1 credit): phrase filter F_c = title_and_abstract.search:(\"alias1\" OR \"alias2\") (URL-encoded, quoted phrases OR-joined); /works?filter=F_c,type:article|review,is_paratext:false&group_by=publication_year -> n_c[y]. t0 = first y in 2000..2014 with n_c[y] >= 20 (none -> drop, reason 'no_onset'). newborn = all(n_c[t0-k] < 0.25*n_c[t0+2] for k in 1..3).\n\n4. VENUE FIELDS + HOME. For each c: group_by=primary_location.source.id with F_c and publication_year:t0-(t0+1) (1 credit; cursor-page if meta.groups_count > 200, at most 2 pages). SOURCE FIELD LOOKUP for every new source ID (global cache source_field.json): free singleton GET /sources/{id}?select=id,display_name,topics (fall back to a full singleton if select is rejected; fall back to 50-ID OR batches at 1 credit only if singletons are rate-limited). field(source) = the OpenAlex field (26-level; topics[].field.id / display_name) holding >= 40% of the source's summed topic counts, else 'unlabelled'. home(c) = the set of fields with >= 40% of labelled papers in t0..t0+1 (if none, the modal field). DEV RESTRICTION: DEV_FIELDS = {Computer Science, Engineering, Biochemistry Genetics and Molecular Biology, Medicine}. Keep c iff home(c) is a subset of DEV_FIELDS AND 2003 <= t0 <= 2009. Otherwise DROP, log it to dropped_concepts.csv with the reason, and make NO further calls for c (held-out fields are sealed). group(c) = home field (if multi-home within the dev fields, take the field with the most labelled papers).\n\n5. OUTCOME + EARLY-FIELD COUNTS for each dev concept (about 3-7 credits): group_by=primary_location.source.id for windows W_early = t0..t0+4 and W_out = t0+6..t0+8, using cursor paging with at most 5 pages per window (groups come sorted by count). The remainder (meta.count minus the listed sum) counts as unlabelled. Map sources to fields. Also yearly counts come from step 3. Compute:\n   O2r(m=30) = sum_j [1 - C(N-n_j,30)/C(N,30)] over the labelled field counts n_j in W_out, N = sum n_j (use scipy.special.gammaln for log-binomials). If N < 30, O2r = NaN and hurdle flag = 1. Also compute O2r at m=50.\n   O1 = 1 if mean_{y in t0+6..t0+8} n_c[y]/total_works[y] >= n_c[t0+5]/total_works[t0+5], else 0.\n   O3 = 1 if argmax_{y in t0+3..t0+8} n_c[y] exists AND peak/mean(n_c[t0+7], n_c[t0+8]) >= 2 (peak = max over t0+3..t0+8; follow the S0 wording literally), else 0.\n   Early fields: share_j over labelled papers in W_early; off-home fields = labelled fields not in home(c).\n   R_j for each off-home j with >= 5 labelled papers in W_early: R_j = 1 if share_j(W_out) >= 0.5*share_j(W_early) AND count_j(W_out)/3 >= 3, else 0.\n   B5: log1p(sum n_c[t0..t0+4]); growth = log((n_c[t0+4]+1)/(n_c[t0+1]+1)); off-home share (W_early, labelled); Shannon entropy over labelled early fields; number of early fields with >= 2 papers. Field baseline: log1p(count_j early), growth_j = log((count_j in t0+3..t0+4 + 1)/(count_j in t0..t0+1 + 1)), which needs two extra group_by windows ONLY IF credits allow; otherwise growth_j = NaN, is dropped from B_field, and this is noted. Also share_j early. Label coverage = labelled/total per window.\n\n6. EARLY PAPERS (10 credits per concept): /works?filter=F_c,type:article|review,is_paratext:false,publication_year:t0-(t0+4)&sample=200&seed=<20260928 + index of c>&per_page=200&select=id,publication_year,authorships,primary_location,title,abstract_inverted_index,referenced_works_count. If the concept has <= 200 early works, the sample is the population (take all). For papers with is_authors_truncated=true, fetch the full authorships via free singleton /works/{id}?select=id,authorships. Paper venue field = field(primary_location.source.id) (unknown if the source is missing or unlabelled). Local phrase check: rebuild the abstract from the inverted index; exact = case-insensitive exact alias match in title+abstract; lemma = the same after simple plural/hyphen normalisation. Store exact_flag and record the stemmed-to-exact share. Record abstract availability share and mean referenced_works_count as coverage moderators.\n\n7. SOCIAL FEATURES (src/social.py), per concept, using papers P (all stemmed matches = primary; exact-confirmed = sensitivity):\n   author key = authorships[].author.id (drop authorships without an ID and count them). Paper label: home / off-home / unknown by venue field. Adopter set A = authors on P; off-home adopters A_off = authors on at least one off-home paper; home adopters A_home similarly.\n   def U_of(papers): build graph G on all authors of `papers`, with an edge between every co-author pair on each paper (for a paper with k > 50 authors, add a star from the first author instead of a clique for speed, since connectivity is identical). comps = connected components. A_off' = off-home adopters within `papers`. If |A_off'| < 5, return NaN. Otherwise return |{C in comps : C ∩ A_off' non-empty}| / |A_off'|.\n   PRIMARY U = mean over 30 seeded draws of U_of(random subset of m=80 papers from P). If |P| < 80, use U_of(P) once and set u_small=1. Sensitivity: U_m150 (only concepts with |P| >= 150), U_full = U_of(P), U_exact (P restricted to exact matches, m = min(80,|P_exact|)), U_nohyper (drop papers with > 25 authors), U_namemerge (author key = normalised surname + first initial).\n   SECONDARIES (all on the same m=80 draws, averaged): K_off = number of components touching A_off'; LCC_share = largest component's share of A_off'; U_induced = components of the subgraph induced on A_off' / |A_off'|; bridge_share = share of A_off' who co-authored (on the drawn papers) with at least one home adopter; per-field K_j and U_j for each off-home field j (on the full P); Chao1 of groups: treat components touching A_off' as species, with abundance = the number of off-home papers each component appears on; S_chao1 = S_obs + f1*(f1-1)/(2*(f2+1)), reported raw and divided by |A_off'|; foils: log |A|, log |A_off|, mean and median team size, share of papers with > 25 authors.\n   CONFIGURATION NULL (secondary U_z): on each m=80 draw, build the author-paper bipartite graph and run 200 degree-preserving random rewirings (each paper keeps its team size and each author keeps their paper count, e.g. repeated random swaps of author stubs between papers, avoiding duplicate authors on a paper; about 10 x edges swaps). Keep paper labels fixed. U_z = (U_obs - mean U_null)/sd U_null, averaged over draws (use 10 draws x 100 nulls if runtime demands).\n   TEMPORAL (for RQ2 next iteration; no screen use): yearly U_t on cumulative papers up to t for t0..t0+4, at m = min(80, cumulative papers).\n\n8. RELIABILITY (criterion iii): for s in 1..50: split P randomly into halves P1, P2 (paper level). U1 = mean of 10 draws of U_of(40 papers from P1), and U2 likewise from P2 (use all papers if a half has fewer than 40). r_s = Spearman over concepts (U1, U2), with concepts missing either value excluded. Report median r_s, its Spearman-Brown value 2r/(1+r), and the 5-95% range. PRIOR-TIE CHECK (budget-adaptive; target 15 concepts, expand to 25 if credits remain after steps 2-8, minimum 10). Take the first dev concepts in seeded order with |A_off| >= 30. Draw 100 off-home adopters (seeded). For the 2 OR-batches of 50 author IDs: /works?filter=authorships.author.id:A1|...|A50,publication_year:(t0-5)-(t0-1),type:article|review&select=id,authorships&per_page=200 with cursor paging, at most 4 pages per batch (<= 8 credits per concept; no search, so 1 credit per page). Add prior co-authorship edges ONLY between the 100 sampled adopters. U_prior = components (over the 100 sampled adopters, early edges + prior edges) / 100 versus U_noprior = the same without prior edges. SCREEN (src/screen.py). The dev table D has one row per dev concept, with B5, U and group. Primary analysis set: concepts with non-missing O2r and U. Report the count.\n   LOGO: for g in 4 groups: train on the other 3 groups, fit StandardScaler + Ridge(alpha=1) for [B5] and [B5+U], and predict group g, giving pooled out-of-fold (OOF) predictions. Record U's standardized coefficient and sign in each fold.\n   Delta_rho = Spearman(OOF_B5+U, O2r) - Spearman(OOF_B5, O2r). Per-group gain = the same difference computed within g (report the n per group; groups with n < 6 are reported as 'insufficient').\n   CI: 2,000 bootstrap resamples of concept rows of the (O2r, OOF_B5, OOF_B5+U) table (predictions fixed); 90% percentile CI of Delta_rho. X_field = [log count_j early, growth_j (if available), share_j early]; the candidate adds U_j (NaN if the field has < 5 adopters, then median-impute + indicator). Ideas become 'core concepts' when they reach 'expansive networks of unrelated authors', achieve consistent usage, co-occur with prominent ideas and fit existing traditions. I read the abstract and the publisher summary only; the full text was paywalled (HTTP 403), so the exact operationalisation of 'unrelated authors' is NOT verified here and the executor must not claim an exact replication. Bettencourt, Kaiser & Kaur (2009, Journal of Informetrics 3(3):210-221, 'Scientific discovery and topological transitions in collaboration networks') is the opposing structural expectation: across 8 fields, a field's co-authorship graph goes from many small disconnected groups to a giant connected component as the field matures ('shared concepts and tools knit practitioners together'). Cheng et al. therefore predict that MORE independent groups early on means wider later spread, while Bettencourt et al. predict that consolidation (fewer groups, a giant component) accompanies successful field formation. The screen must let the data choose the sign and report it per fold. Other standard background: Newman (2001, PNAS) on co-authorship network structure; Rotolo, Hicks & Martin (2015, Research Policy) on the five attributes of emergence and the lack of a ground truth; Small, Boyack & Klavans (2014, Research Policy) on emerging-topic detection with citation/co-occurrence structure. (2) Baselines every comparable paper reports: raw volume and growth of the concept, and the number of distinct adopting authors. Any component-count measure scales almost mechanically with adopter count and team size, so readers expect at minimum a size control (adopter count, papers) and preferably a null that preserves team sizes and author productivity. Mean team size is a known confounder: large-team fields (genomics consortia, clinical trials) have few, very large components. Compute:\\n   O2r(m=30) = sum_j [1 - C(N-n_j,30)/C(N,30)] over the labelled field counts n_j in W_out, N = sum n_j (use scipy.special.gammaln for log-binomials). If N < 30, O2r = NaN and hurdle flag = 1. Also compute O2r at m=50.\\n   O1 = 1 if mean_{y in t0+6..t0+8} n_c[y]/total_works[y] >= n_c[t0+5]/total_works[t0+5], else 0.\\n   O3 = 1 if argmax_{y in t0+3..t0+8} n_c[y] exists AND peak/mean(n_c[t0+7], n_c[t0+8]) >= 2 (peak = max over t0+3..t0+8; follow the S0 wording literally), else 0.\\n   Early fields: share_j over labelled papers in W_early; off-home fields = labelled fields not in home(c).\\n   R_j for each off-home j with >= 5 labelled papers in W_early: R_j = 1 if share_j(W_out) >= 0.5*share_j(W_early) AND count_j(W_out)/3 >= 3, else 0.\\n   B5: log1p(sum n_c[t0..t0+4]); growth = log((n_c[t0+4]+1)/(n_c[t0+1]+1)); off-home share (W_early, labelled); Shannon entropy over labelled early fields; number of early fields with >= 2 papers. Field baseline: log1p(count_j early), growth_j = log((count_j in t0+3..t0+4 + 1)/(count_j in t0..t0+1 + 1)), which needs two extra group_by windows ONLY IF credits allow; otherwise growth_j = NaN, is dropped from B_field, and this is noted. Also share_j early. Label coverage = labelled/total per window.\\n\\n6. EARLY PAPERS (10 credits per concept): /works?filter=F_c,type:article|review,is_paratext:false,publication_year:t0-(t0+4)&sample=200&seed=<20260928 + index of c>&per_page=200&select=id,publication_year,authorships,primary_location,title,abstract_inverted_index,referenced_works_count. If the concept has <= 200 early works, the sample is the population (take all). For papers with is_authors_truncated=true, fetch the full authorships via free singleton /works/{id}?select=id,authorships. Paper venue field = field(primary_location.source.id) (unknown if the source is missing or unlabelled). Local phrase check: rebuild the abstract from the inverted index; exact = case-insensitive exact alias match in title+abstract; lemma = the same after simple plural/hyphen normalisation. Store exact_flag and record the stemmed-to-exact share. Record abstract availability share and mean referenced_works_count as coverage moderators.\\n\\n7. SOCIAL FEATURES (src/social.py), per concept, using papers P (all stemmed matches = primary; exact-confirmed = sensitivity):\\n   author key = authorships[].author.id (drop authorships without an ID and count them). Paper label: home / off-home / unknown by venue field. Adopter set A = authors on P; off-home adopters A_off = authors on at least one off-home paper; home adopters A_home similarly.\\n   def U_of(papers): build graph G on all authors of `papers`, with an edge between every co-author pair on each paper (for a paper with k > 50 authors, add a star from the first author instead of a clique for speed, since connectivity is identical). comps = connected components. A_off' = off-home adopters within `papers`. If |A_off'| < 5, return NaN. Otherwise return |{C in comps : C ∩ A_off' non-empty}| / |A_off'|.\\n   PRIMARY U = mean over 30 seeded draws of U_of(random subset of m=80 papers from P). If |P| < 80, use U_of(P) once and set u_small=1. Sensitivity: U_m150 (only concepts with |P| >= 150), U_full = U_of(P), U_exact (P restricted to exact matches, m = min(80,|P_exact|)), U_nohyper (drop papers with > 25 authors), U_namemerge (author key = normalised surname + first initial).\\n   SECONDARIES (all on the same m=80 draws, averaged): K_off = number of components touching A_off'; LCC_share = largest component's share of A_off'; U_induced = components of the subgraph induced on A_off' / |A_off'|; bridge_share = share of A_off' who co-authored (on the drawn papers) with at least one home adopter; per-field K_j and U_j for each off-home field j (on the full P); Chao1 of groups: treat components touching A_off' as species, with abundance = the number of off-home papers each component appears on; S_chao1 = S_obs + f1*(f1-1)/(2*(f2+1)), reported raw and divided by |A_off'|; foils: log |A|, log |A_off|, mean and median team size, share of papers with > 25 authors.\\n   CONFIGURATION NULL (secondary U_z): on each m=80 draw, build the author-paper bipartite graph and run 200 degree-preserving random rewirings (each paper keeps its team size and each author keeps their paper count, e.g. repeated random swaps of author stubs between papers, avoiding duplicate authors on a paper; about 10 x edges swaps). Keep paper labels fixed. U_z = (U_obs - mean U_null)/sd U_null, averaged over draws (use 10 draws x 100 nulls if runtime demands).\\n   TEMPORAL (for RQ2 next iteration; no screen use): yearly U_t on cumulative papers up to t for t0..t0+4, at m = min(80, cumulative papers).\\n\\n8. RELIABILITY (criterion iii): for s in 1..50: split P randomly into halves P1, P2 (paper level). U1 = mean of 10 draws of U_of(40 papers from P1), and U2 likewise from P2 (use all papers if a half has fewer than 40). r_s = Spearman over concepts (U1, U2), with concepts missing either value excluded. Report median r_s, its Spearman-Brown value 2r/(1+r), and the 5-95% range. Also report |Spearman(U, log early volume)| and |Spearman(U, early growth)| (criterion iv) over the dev concepts, plus Spearman(U, log |A_off|) and Spearman(U, mean team size) as extra diagnostics.\\n\\n9. PRIOR-TIE CHECK (budget-adaptive; target 15 concepts, expand to 25 if credits remain after steps 2-8, minimum 10). Take the first dev concepts in seeded order with |A_off| >= 30. Draw 100 off-home adopters (seeded). For the 2 OR-batches of 50 author IDs: /works?filter=authorships.author.id:A1|...|A50,publication_year:(t0-5)-(t0-1),type:article|review&select=id,authorships&per_page=200 with cursor paging, at most 4 pages per batch (<= 8 credits per concept; no search, so 1 credit per page). Add prior co-authorship edges ONLY between the 100 sampled adopters. U_prior = components (over the 100 sampled adopters, early edges + prior edges) / 100 versus U_noprior = the same without prior edges. Report the Spearman across concepts with a bootstrap 90% CI, the mean ratio U_prior/U_noprior, and whether rho >= 0.7 (cheap version adequate). Flag if the 4-page cap truncated a batch.\\n\\n10. SCREEN (src/screen.py). The dev table D has one row per dev concept, with B5, U and group. Primary analysis set: concepts with non-missing O2r and U. Missing U: impute the training-fold median and add a missing indicator ONLY in a sensitivity version. Report the count.\\n   LOGO: for g in 4 groups: train on the other 3 groups, fit StandardScaler + Ridge(alpha=1) for [B5] and [B5+U], and predict group g, giving pooled out-of-fold (OOF) predictions. Record U's standardized coefficient and sign in each fold.\\n   Delta_rho = Spearman(OOF_B5+U, O2r) - Spearman(OOF_B5, O2r). Per-group gain = the same difference computed within g (report the n per group; groups with n < 6 are reported as 'insufficient').\\n   CI: 2,000 bootstrap resamples of concept rows of the (O2r, OOF_B5, OOF_B5+U) table (predictions fixed); 90% percentile CI of Delta_rho. Sensitivity: 200 resamples with refitting inside LOGO.\\n   O1 and O3: identical LOGO with LogisticRegression(C=1.0, L2, standardized); AUC difference with a bootstrap CI (skip a fold if the left-out group has a single class; report it).\\n   Newborn-only sensitivity, m=50 O2r sensitivity, and hurdle for the N<30 concepts: logistic on the hurdle flag, [B5] vs [B5+U], AUC delta (descriptive).\\n   Single-feature table: out-of-fold Spearman of each secondary alone and of B5 + each secondary (descriptive, NOT used by the rule).\\n   FIELD LEVEL: rows = (concept, off-home field j) with R_j defined. X_field = [log count_j early, growth_j (if available), share_j early]; the candidate adds U_j (NaN if the field has < 5 adopters, then median-impute + indicator). LOGO by the concept's group; LogisticRegression; AUC(B_field) vs AUC(B_field + U_j); 2,000 concept-clustered bootstrap resamples (resample concepts, take all their rows) for a 90% CI.\\n   MODERATORS (the 'low-coverage fields' claim): split the dev concepts at the median of the mean referenced_works_count and of the abstract-availability share, and report the per-concept OOF gain |O2r - OOF_B5| - |O2r - OOF_B5+U| by moderator half. The actual comparison against lineage is deferred to the joined analysis next iteration (lineage features come from sibling experiment 1).\\n   RULE (pre-registered, apply mechanically): survives = (Delta_rho >= 0.10) AND (CI_lo > 0) AND (#groups with positive gain >= 3) AND (SB reliability >= 0.6) AND (|rho_vol| <= 0.6) AND (|rho_growth| <= 0.6). Write each criterion's value and pass/fail.\\n\\n11. OUTPUTS (workspace root): outcomes.csv (concept, aliases, t0, newborn, home, group, label_cov_early, label_cov_out, O1, O2r, O2r_m50, O3, hurdle); field_outcomes.csv (concept, field, R_j, log_count_early, growth_j, share_early, U_j); features.csv (concept + U and every secondary/sensitivity column above + B5 columns + coverage moderators + u_small + n_papers_sampled + n_authors_noid); features_temporal.csv (concept, year, U_t); dropped_concepts.csv; screen_result.json (Delta_rho, CI, per-group gains and n, fold coefficients and signs, reliability stats, volume/growth correlations, O1/O3 AUC deltas, field-level AUC delta and CI, prior-tie results, sensitivities, n used, rule verdict, credits spent by kind, the measured cost table); credit_ledger.jsonl (no key). PIPELINE FILES: method_out.json in exp_gen_sol_out format with 2 dataset groups. (a) 'p78_dev_concepts': one example per dev concept; input = JSON string {concept, aliases, t0, home, B5, U}; output = str(O2r) ('NA' for the hurdle); metadata_fold = group name; metadata_newborn; predict_baseline_B5 = str(OOF_B5); predict_B5_plus_U = str(OOF_B5+U). (b) 'concept_field_retention': one example per (concept, j); output = str(R_j); predict_baseline_Bfield and predict_Bfield_plus_Uj = OOF probabilities. Then full_method_out.json, mini_method_out.json and preview_method_out.json via the aii-json skill format script, and reproducibility.md (commands, seeds, credit spend, cache location). Validate with aii-json. The full file must hold >= 50 examples (the dev and field rows together easily exceed this).\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"CREDITS. The projected spend is about 1 (denominator) + 78 (grounding) + about 85 (home) + about 60x5 (windows) + about 60x10 (early samples) + about 120 (prior ties), roughly 1,180. If after step 4 the projection exceeds 1,150: (1) drop the growth_j windows (field baseline without growth); (2) cap outcome-window paging at 3 pages; (3) shrink the prior-tie check to 10 concepts; (4) if it is still over, process dev concepts in the seeded order until the cap is reached and report the achieved n (the seeded order makes the subset unbiased). Stop immediately if the shared header shows remaining < 1,000 and write partial outputs with a 'partial' flag. If the measured costs differ from the model (e.g. group_by with search is charged as a search), re-project, and prefer cutting the prior-tie check and paging over cutting concepts. API. If `sample`+`seed` is rejected together with the search filter, use per_page=200, page=1 in default order (not sorted by citations, which would bias towards influential papers), and flag sample_mode='first_page'. If select on /sources is rejected, use the full singleton. If singletons get throttled, use 50-ID OR batches at 1 credit. If more than 20% of authorships lack an author ID for a concept, use the surname + first-initial key as the primary for that concept and flag it. FEATURE. If U is volume-bound (|rho| > 0.6 with log volume), do NOT swap in another primary (the rule is pre-registered). Report the failure, and report U_z and U_m150 as the size-free variants the next iteration can consider. If fewer than 40 dev concepts remain with both U and O2r, still run everything, label the result 'underpowered' and give the width of the CI. If a left-out group has fewer than 6 concepts, report its gain as 'insufficient' and count it as NOT positive for criterion (ii) (conservative). If the configuration null is too slow, use 10 draws x 100 swaps-nulls, or python-igraph. If the prior-tie Spearman < 0.7, state that the cheap U is not an adequate proxy and estimate the credits a full prior-tie version would need for confirmation. If the fold coefficient signs disagree (positive in some groups, negative in others), report this as a domain-specific (non-generalising) result rather than averaging it away.\",\n  \"testing_plan\": \"T0 UNIT TESTS (no API, run first). (a) U_of on toy graphs: 3 disjoint off-home papers with 2 authors each gives U = 3/6 = 0.5. Chain papers sharing authors merge into 1 component. An off-home adopter linked to another off-home adopter via a home author counts as 1 group. Fewer than 5 off-home adopters gives NaN. (b) The star shortcut for k > 50 authors gives the same components as the clique. (c) O2r hypergeometric formula vs Monte-Carlo rarefaction (10k draws) on a random count vector: agreement within 0.02; N < 30 gives NaN. (d) The configuration-null rewiring preserves every paper's team size and every author's degree exactly (assert). (e) Screen harness on synthetic data (60 concepts, 4 groups): a feature equal to O2r plus noise must give Delta_rho > 0.1 with CI_lo > 0; a pure-noise feature must give |Delta_rho| < 0.05 on average over 20 seeds; a feature that works only in one group must fail criterion (ii). (f) Split-half on synthetic data with known reliability recovers it within 0.1. T1 COST SMOKE (<= 15 credits): run the denominator, grounding and home steps for the first concept in the seeded order, print the header costs per call kind, compare them with the model (group_by 1, search page 10, singleton 0) and write cost_check.json. Abort on a >2x mismatch. T2 MINI (first 5 concepts in the seeded order that pass the dev restriction; about 90 credits): the full pipeline end to end. Check that the t0 values look plausible against known history (e.g. RNA-seq around 2008-2009, cloud computing around 2008, iPS cells around 2007-2008). Print the label coverage, the stemmed-to-exact share, |P|, |A_off| and U. Sanity expectation: consortium-heavy concepts (GWAS, next-generation sequencing) should have larger mean team sizes and lower U than small-team CS concepts. If the opposite holds, inspect the author-ID handling before continuing. Confirm that the cache prevents re-billing on a rerun (the ledger shows 0 new credits). T3 20 CONCEPTS: check the distribution of U (no mass at 0 or 1), the correlation with log volume (early warning for criterion iv), the NaN rates, and the credit burn per concept against the projection; adjust the fallback levers if needed. T4 ALL: run the rest within the cap, then the reliability, prior-tie and screen steps. FINAL CHECKS: every dev concept appears in outcomes.csv and features.csv; no dropped concept has any paid call after step 4 (verify with the ledger); screen_result.json lists all rule criteria with pass/fail; the method_out.json files validate against exp_gen_sol_out with >= 50 examples; the file sizes are checked (ls -lh); the api key is absent from every output (grep).\"\n}\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}

# GEN_PLAN iteration 1, experiment 2: independent author groups as a spread signal

This is the plan for candidate S from the iteration-1 wide screen. Nothing was executed here: no data was downloaded and no code was run.

## What the plan does
- **Primary feature.** Measure U on the P78 dev panel. U is the number of early co-authorship components that contain off-home adopters, divided by the number of off-home adopters. It is rarefied at m = 80 randomly drawn early papers, so it does not just track volume.
- **Screen.** U is scored with the shared S0 protocol and the pre-registered selection rule: leave-one-field-group-out ridge, Delta-rho for O2r, bootstrap CI, split-half reliability and volume correlations. A field-level U_j -> R_j test is added.
- **Budget fix.** The strategy's sampling was changed to fit the 1,200-credit cap. Measured OpenAlex prices are 10 credits per 200-work search page, 1 credit per `group_by` or filter-only page, and free singletons. So early papers are sampled at most 200 per concept, and the prior-tie check is filter-only and budget-adaptive.
- **Literature framing.** Cheng et al. (2023, ASR) predict that more unrelated author groups early on means wider later spread. Bettencourt et al. (2009, J. Informetrics) predict that a giant component forms as a field succeeds. The screen reports the sign of U's effect per fold instead of assuming one.

## Layout
- `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`: the plan (ExperimentPlan schema).
- `.aii/manifest.yaml`: storage manifest (empty; nothing heavy).
```

```
I've written the plan for artifact 2 of the strategy: testing whether early adoption by many unconnected groups of co-authors predicts that a concept later spreads widely across fields. Nothing was executed; no data was downloaded and no code was run. I haven't parsed the plan file to confirm it is valid JSON, because I couldn't run code here.

Your message reached me cut off: it started mid-sentence at "with citations from the related work…". So I recovered the full task instructions and output format from this run's logs and the pipeline's prompt templates. I also used the strategy step's direction for this artifact.

**What the plan measures.** For each concept, it builds the co-authorship network of authors who used the concept in its first five years. The main measure (U) is the number of separate author groups containing authors from outside the concept's home field, divided by the number of such authors. It is scored against the same simple count baseline, outcomes and fixed selection rule as the other four candidates, on the same 78-concept panel.

**Changes from the strategy's direction:**
- **OpenAlex budget.** Searching for a concept's papers costs 10 credits per 200 works (per OpenAlex's pricing page and the run's own earlier measurements). The strategy's 800-papers-per-concept limit would have cost about 2,600 credits against this artifact's 1,200-credit cap. The plan instead takes a random sample of at most 200 early papers per concept and looks up journals individually (free) or in batches (1 credit each).
- **Pre-existing collaborations.** The strategy asked for 25 concepts at up to 40 credits each (about 1,000 credits). The plan cuts this to 10–15 concepts at 8 credits or less each. With so few concepts, the result only indicates whether the cheap version of U is good enough.
- **Size bias.** A raw group count mostly tracks how many papers a concept has. The plan therefore computes U on a fixed number of randomly drawn papers (80). It also adds a comparison against randomised networks that keep each paper's team size and each author's paper count.
- **Direction of the effect.** Cheng et al. (2023) found that ideas spread when they reach many unrelated authors. Bettencourt et al. (2009) found that successful fields instead merge into one large connected collaboration network. The plan lets the data decide the sign and reports it for each held-out field group.
- **Very large author lists and author identity.** The plan fetches complete author lists for papers with more than 100 authors (list responses cut them off). It adds checks that drop papers with more than 25 authors and that merge authors by surname plus first initial.
- **Held-out fields stay untouched.** Concepts outside the four development fields are dropped before any paid query. Final confirmation relies on this iteration's dataset artifact and on iteration 2.

The plan also lists the files the pipeline requires, a staged test sequence starting with a small cost check, and what to do if credits run short.

Only the abstract of Cheng et al. was accessible (the full text returned "access denied"). The plan therefore tells the executor not to claim an exact replication of their measure.

Files are in `gen_plan_experiment_2/`:
- `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`
- `README.md`
- `.aii/manifest.yaml`

Sources:
- [Cheng et al. 2023, "How New Ideas Diffuse in Science", ASR](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/full/10.1177/00031224231166955)
- [Stanford GSB summary of Cheng et al.](https://www.gsb.stanford.edu/faculty-research/publications/how-new-ideas-diffuse-science)
- [Bettencourt, Kaiser & Kaur 2009, J. Informetrics](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S1751157709000285)
- [OpenAlex example costs](https://help.openalex.org/access/example-costs/)
- [OpenAlex usage-based pricing (CASRAI)](https://casrai.org/news/openalex-api-keys-mandatory-usage-based-pricing-2026)
```
